\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}=V(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}

(S160)

and the variable substitution
\mathbf{r}_{1}\to\mathbf{r}_{1}+\mathbf{R}
, we can rewrite
{\hat{H}}_{W}
as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{W}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\Omega_{\rm tot}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}:n_{f}(\mathbf{r}_{1})\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}aa^{\prime}l}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\cdot(\mathbf{r}_{1}+\mathbf{R})}

\displaystyle\times V(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(\mathbf{k})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta,a^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}):c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:

(S161)

We introduce the integral

\displaystyle X_{aa^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\Omega_{0}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}\ n_{f}(\mathbf{r})\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}V(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{r}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(\mathbf{k})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta,a^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime})

(S162)

such that the interaction can be written as (
e^{i(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}=1
)

{\hat{H}}_{W}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}aa^{\prime}}X_{aa^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{s}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta_{s}s_{2}}:

(S163)

The conduction bands have low energy states around
\mathbf{k}\approx 0
.
For the low energy states we can approximate the integral as
X_{aa^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\approx X_{aa^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(0,0)

\displaystyle X_{aa^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(0,0)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Omega_{0}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}n_{f}(\mathbf{r})\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}V(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})e^{i(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{r}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta,a^{\prime}}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Omega_{0}}\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{G}}n_{f}(\mathbf{G})V(\mathbf{G})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{G}\beta,a^{\prime}}(0)\ .

(S164)

Here
\mathbf{Q}
indexes all vectors in
\mathcal{Q}_{+}\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{-}
and
\mathbf{G}
indexes reciprocal lattices.
Under the crystalline symmetries the function
n_{f}(\mathbf{G})V(\mathbf{G})
forms an identity representation, thus the summation over
\mathbf{Q},\mathbf{G},\beta
is non-vanishing only if
\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{G}\beta,a^{\prime}}(0)
also form an identity representation of the valley-preserving symmetries.
To be concrete, because
n_{f}(g\mathbf{G})V(g\mathbf{G})=n_{f}(\mathbf{G})V(\mathbf{G})
for any crystalline symmetry
g
that preserves the valley, we can rewrite the
X
matrix as

\displaystyle X_{aa^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(0,0)=\frac{1}{\Omega_{0}}\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{G}}n_{f}(g\mathbf{G})V(g\mathbf{G})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{G}\beta,a^{\prime}}(0)\ ,

(S165)

Relabeling
\mathbf{Q}\to g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}
,
\mathbf{G}\to g^{-1}\mathbf{G}
, we obtain

\displaystyle X_{aa^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(0,0)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Omega_{0}}\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\mathbf{G}}n_{f}(\mathbf{G})V(\mathbf{G})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}-g^{-1}\mathbf{G}\beta,a^{\prime}}(0)\ .

(S166)

For simplicity, we denote the symmetry operator matrices in
eqs.

S16
and
S17
as
D_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha\eta,\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}(g)=\delta_{\mathbf{Q},g\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\overline{D}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(g)
for
g
that preserve the valley.
Since
\overline{D}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(g)
’s are unitary,
i.e.
,
\sum_{\alpha}\overline{D}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(\eta)*}(g)\overline{D}_{\alpha,\beta^{\prime}}^{(\eta)}(g)=\delta_{\beta\beta^{\prime}}
, there are

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta\mathbf{Q}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}-g^{-1}\mathbf{G}\beta,a^{\prime}}(0)=\sum_{\beta\beta^{\prime}\mathbf{Q}}\sum_{\alpha}\overline{D}_{\alpha,\beta}^{(\eta_{2})*}(g)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\overline{D}_{\alpha,\beta^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}-g^{-1}\mathbf{G}\beta^{\prime},a^{\prime}}(0)

(S167)

Since the vectors
\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}
form irreducible representations given by
section

S2.2
,
i.e.
,

\sum_{\beta}\overline{D}_{\alpha,\beta^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(g)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}\beta^{\prime},a}(0)=\sum_{\beta\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}}{D}_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha\eta_{2},\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}\eta_{2}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}\beta^{\prime},a}(0)=\sum_{b}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,a^{\prime}}(0)D_{b\eta_{2},a\eta_{2}}^{(c)}(g)

(S168)

there is

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta\mathbf{Q}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{g^{-1}\mathbf{Q}-g^{-1}\mathbf{G}\beta,a^{\prime}}(0)=\sum_{bb^{\prime}}\sum_{\alpha\mathbf{Q}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,b}(0)D_{b\eta_{2},a\eta_{2}}^{(c)*}(g)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{G}\alpha,b^{\prime}}(0)D_{b^{\prime}\eta_{2},a^{\prime}\eta_{2}}^{(c)}(g)

(S169)

Substituting this relation into
eq.

S166
, we obtain

\displaystyle X_{aa^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(0,0)=

\displaystyle\sum_{bb^{\prime}}D_{b\eta_{2},a\eta_{2}}^{(c)*}(g)X_{bb^{\prime}}^{(\eta_{2})}(0,0)D_{b^{\prime}\eta_{2},a^{\prime}\eta_{2}}^{(c)}(g)\ .

(S170)

In other words,
X
commutes with all
D^{(c)}(g)
for crystalline symmetries
g
that preserve the valley.
These symmetries are generated by
g=C_{3z}
,
C_{2x}
,
C_{2z}T
.
Since
D^{(c)}(g)
consists of three different irreducible representations of the crystalline symmetry,
i.e.
,
\Gamma_{3}\oplus\Gamma_{1}\oplus\Gamma_{2}
,
X
must take the following form in the
\Gamma_{3}\oplus\Gamma_{1}\oplus\Gamma_{2}
basis according to the Schur’s lemma

X^{(\eta)}=\begin{pmatrix}W_{1}^{(\eta)}&0&0&0\\
0&W_{1}^{(\eta)}&0&0\\
0&0&W_{3}^{(\eta)}&W_{3}^{\prime(\eta)}\\
0&0&W_{3}^{\prime(\eta)}&W_{3}^{(\eta)}\end{pmatrix}

(S171)

The off diagonal term
W_{3}^{\prime(\eta)}
is allowed by the crystalline symmetries because the third and fourth components are mixed states of the
\Gamma_{1}\oplus\Gamma_{2}
basis.
To be concrete, in the
\Gamma_{1}\oplus\Gamma_{2}
subspace in each valley
\eta
,
C_{3z}
,
C_{2z}
, and
C_{2z}T
are represented by
\sigma_{0}
,
\sigma_{x}
,
\sigma_{x}K
(with
K
being complex conjugation), respectively, and hence
W_{3}^{(\eta)}\sigma_{0}+W_{3}^{\prime(\eta)}\sigma_{x}
commutes with all of them.
However,
W_{3}^{\prime(\eta)}
is forbidden by the particle-hole symmetry
P
.
Due to
[X^{(\eta)},D^{(c,\eta)}(P)]=0
and
D^{(c,\eta)}_{aa^{\prime}}(P)=D^{(c)}_{a\eta,a^{\prime}\eta}(g)=\eta[(-i\sigma_{z})\oplus(-i\sigma_{z})]_{aa^{\prime}}
,
W_{3}^{\prime(\eta)}
must vanish and hence
X^{(\eta)}
is diagonal.
Since
n_{f}(\mathbf{r})
is a real function, there must be
n_{f}(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{Q}^{\prime})=n_{f}^{*}(\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q})
and hence
X^{(\eta)}_{aa^{\prime}}(0,0)
is a hermitian matrix, implying that
X^{(\eta)}_{aa}(0,0)
are all real numbers.
Due to the time-reversal symmetry,
X^{(\eta)}_{aa^{\prime}}(0,0)
does not depend on the
\eta
index.
We hence write the
X
matrix as
X^{(\eta)}_{aa^{\prime}}(0,0)=W_{a}\delta_{aa^{\prime}}
, where
W_{1}=W_{2}
,
W_{3}=W_{4}
.
For
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.8
we find
W_{1}=44.03
meV and
W_{3}=50.20
meV.
Values of
W_{a}
at other
w_{0}/w_{1}
are tabulated in
table

S6
.

In summary, the density-density interaction between the local orbitals and the conduction bands can be written as

{\hat{H}}_{W}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}a}W_{a}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}:

(S172)

S3.2.3
Density-density interaction of the conduction bands (the second term)

We third consider the density-density interaction of the conduction bands

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{V}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}^{2}}\sum_{\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{1}\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})

\displaystyle\times e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}\beta_{2},a_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})

\displaystyle\times:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}a_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:\ .

(S173)

After integration over
\mathbf{r}_{1}
, there is

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{V}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}^{2}}\sum_{\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{1}\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1})

\displaystyle\times e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}^{\prime}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}\beta_{2},a_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})

\displaystyle\times:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}a_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:\ .

(S174)

Integration over
\mathbf{r}_{2}
leads to the momentum conservation
\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}=\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}
.
Since
-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}
and
-\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}
are reciprocal lattices and
\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{k}_{1}
,
\mathbf{k}_{2}
,
\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}
are small momenta around
\Gamma_{M}
, there must be
\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{1}=\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{2}
and
-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}=-\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}
separately.
We introduce the momentum
\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{1}=\mathbf{k}_{2}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{2}
, the reciprocal lattice
\mathbf{G}=-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}=-\mathbf{Q}_{2}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}
and rewrite the interaction as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{V}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N}\sum_{\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{q}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}V(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})

\displaystyle\times\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{G}\beta_{1},a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{G}\beta_{2},a_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}):c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}a_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:\ .

(S175)

We introduce the matrix

X_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2};\mathbf{q})=\frac{1}{\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}V(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})\langle\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})\rangle\langle\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})\rangle

(S176)

such that the interaction can be simplified to

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{V}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{q}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}X_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2};\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle\qquad\times:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}a_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:\ .

(S177)

For low energy states around
\mathbf{k}=0
, we can approximate the
X
matrix by replacing
\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{a}(\mathbf{k})
with
\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{a}(0)

X_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2};\mathbf{q})\approx X_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}(0,0;\mathbf{q})=\frac{1}{\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}V(\mathbf{q}+\mathbf{G})\langle\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(0)|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{G})\rangle\langle\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{G})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(0)\rangle\ .

(S178)

We find that the summation over
\mathbf{G}
is dominated by the
\mathbf{G}=0
component.
For example, the diagonal element
X_{\eta_{1}11,\eta_{2}11}(0,0;0)
is found to be 49.22meV, and the
\mathbf{G}=0
component is
\frac{1}{\Omega_{0}}V(0)=48.33
meV.
Therefore, it is reasonable to keep only the
\mathbf{G}=0
component.
Then we can write
{\hat{H}}_{V}
as

{\hat{H}}_{V}=\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}a_{1}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}a_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{q}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}V(\mathbf{q}):c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:\ .

(S179)

S3.3
Exchange terms (the tenth term)

S3.3.1
Constraints of the coupling matrix

Now we consider the
:\hat{\rho}_{fc}:V:\hat{\rho}_{cf}:+(\leftrightarrow)
interaction discussed in
section

S3.1
(the tenth term)

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}\\
\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R}_{1}\alpha_{1}\\
\mathbf{R}_{2}\alpha_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}\ V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})

\displaystyle\times w^{(\eta_{1})*}_{l_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R}_{1})w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})e^{i\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{1}+i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{-i\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}-i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(f_{\mathbf{R}_{1}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}_{1}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\right)

(S180)

Now we argue that, since the Wannier functions are well localized, the
\mathbf{R}_{1}=\mathbf{R}_{2}
terms will be much stronger than the
\mathbf{R}_{1}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}
terms.
In
section

S3.2
we find that the terms
\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}\ V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})n_{f}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R}_{1})n_{f}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R}_{2})
with
\mathbf{R}_{1}=\mathbf{R}_{2}
are about 30 times the terms with
\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}_{2}
being nearest neighbors.
Now
n_{f}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})
is replaced by
w(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})
.
Since
w(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})
(
\sim\sqrt{n(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})}
) has a similar profile as
n_{f}(\mathbf{r}-\mathbf{R})
, the
\mathbf{R}_{1}=\mathbf{R}_{2}
terms will still be much stronger than the
\mathbf{R}_{1}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}
terms.
(We have also numerically verified this argument. We find that, for
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.8
, the largest coupling for
|\mathbf{R}_{1}-\mathbf{R}_{2}|=0
and
|\mathbf{R}_{1}-\mathbf{R}_{2}|=a_{M}
are about 16meV and 1meV, respectively.)
Thus we can simplify this term as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}\\
\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}\ V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})

\displaystyle\times w^{(\eta_{1})*}_{l_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R})w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R})e^{i(\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}-\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\right)

(S181)

Substituting the Fourier transformation of the interaction into the above equation, we obtain

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}\\
\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\ V(\mathbf{q})e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})}

\displaystyle\times w^{(\eta_{1})*}_{l_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R})w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R})e^{i(\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}-\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\right)

(S182)

According to
eq.

S45
, we have

\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R})e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{q})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}=\sqrt{\Omega_{0}}\ e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{q})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{q})\ .

(S183)

\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}w^{(\eta_{1})*}_{l_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{q})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}=\sqrt{\Omega_{0}}\ e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{q})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{q})\ .

(S184)

Therefore, we find

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}\\
\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\ V(\mathbf{q})\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{q})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i(\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}+\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}-\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\right)

(S185)

Since
\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}+\mathbf{Q}_{2}
is a reciprocal lattice vector of the Moire lattice, there is
e^{i(\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}+\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}=1
and hence

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\ V(\mathbf{q})\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\rangle\langle\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})|\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{q})\rangle

\displaystyle\times e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\right)

(S186)

We define the matrix

X_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})=\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\ V(\mathbf{q})\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\rangle\langle\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})|\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{q})\rangle

(S187)

such that the interaction can be written as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1}\\
{\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}X_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle\times\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\right)\ .

(S188)

Since the low energy physics only involves conduction band around
\mathbf{k}_{1,2}\approx 0
, we can approximate the interaction matrix elements by those at
\mathbf{k}_{1}=\mathbf{k}_{2}=0
,
i.e.
,

{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}=X_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}(0,0)=\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\ V(\mathbf{q})\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\alpha_{1}}(-\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(0)\rangle\langle\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(0)|\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}}(-\mathbf{q})\rangle

(S189)

We hence approximate
{\hat{H}}_{J}
as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1}\\
{\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle\times\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\right)\ .

(S190)

After rearranging the creation and annihilation operators,
{\hat{H}}_{J}
can be rewritten as (up to a constant)

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1}\\
{\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\ ,

(S191)

where the normal ordered operators are given in
eq.

S142
.

We now study the
{\mathcal{J}}
matrix (
eq.

S189
).
One may explicitly apply all the crystalline symmetries to
{\mathcal{J}}
as we did around
eq.

S166
to obtain the constraints satisfied by
{\mathcal{J}}
.
Here we adopt a simplified notation, we use
|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{a}(\mathbf{k})\rangle
to represent the vector
\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,a}(\mathbf{k})
and
D(g)|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{a}(\mathbf{k})\rangle
to represent the vector obtained by acting
D(g)
(
eqs.

S29
,
S17
and
S16
) on the vector
|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{a}(\mathbf{k})\rangle
.
According to the
C_{2z}P
representations formed by
f
- and
c
-electrons (
eqs.

S36
and
S65
):

D^{(f)}_{\alpha\eta,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}(C_{2z}P)=-i[\sigma_{y}]_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}[\tau_{y}]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}

(S192)

D^{(c)}_{a\eta,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}(C_{2z}P)=i[\sigma_{y}\oplus\sigma_{y}]_{aa^{\prime}}[\tau_{y}]_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}

(S193)

there are

D(C_{2z}P)|\widetilde{v}^{(\eta)}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{k})\rangle=\sum_{\beta\eta^{\prime}}|\widetilde{v}^{(\eta^{\prime})}_{\beta}(\mathbf{k})\rangle D_{\beta\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}^{(f)}(C_{2z}P)=-e^{i\pi\alpha}e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}\eta}|\widetilde{v}^{(-\eta)}_{\overline{\alpha}}(\mathbf{k})\rangle\ ,

(S194)

D(C_{2z}P)|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{a}(\mathbf{k})\rangle=\sum_{b\eta^{\prime}}|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{a}(\mathbf{k})\rangle D^{(c)}_{b\eta^{\prime},a\eta}(C_{2z}P)=e^{i\pi a}e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}\eta}|\widetilde{u}^{(-\eta)}_{\overline{a}}(\mathbf{k})\rangle,

(S195)

where
\overline{\alpha}=2,1
for
\alpha=1,2
and
\overline{a}=2,1,4,3
for
a=1,2,3,4
.
Inserting
D^{\dagger}(C_{2z}P)D(C_{2z}P)=1
into the inner products in
eq.

S189
, it follows that

{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}=-e^{i\pi(\alpha_{2}-a_{2})}{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},-\eta_{2}\overline{\alpha}_{2}\overline{a}_{2}}=-e^{i\pi(a_{1}-\alpha_{1})}{\mathcal{J}}_{-\eta_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{1}\overline{a}_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}

(S196)

Similarly, due to the
C_{2x}
symmetry (
eqs.

S36
and
S2.2
), the
{\mathcal{J}}
matrix satisfies

{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\overline{\alpha}_{1}\overline{a}_{1},\eta_{2}\overline{\alpha}_{2}\overline{a}_{2}}\ ,

(S197)

due to the
C_{2y}
(
=C_{2x}C_{2z}
) symmetry (
eqs.

S36
and
S2.2
), the
{\mathcal{J}}
matrix satisfies

{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}={\mathcal{J}}_{-\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},-\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}\ ,

(S198)

and due to the
C_{3z}
symmetry (
eqs.

S36
and
S2.2
), the
{\mathcal{J}}
matrix satisfies

{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}=\zeta_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{*}\zeta_{\eta_{1}a_{1}}\zeta_{\eta_{2}a_{2}}^{*}\zeta_{\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}}{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}\ .

(S199)

Here
\zeta_{\eta\alpha}=D^{(f)}_{\alpha\eta,\alpha\eta}(C_{3z})
and
\zeta_{\eta a}=D^{(c)}_{a\eta,a\eta}(C_{3z})
are the
C_{3z}
eigenvalues.

S3.3.2
Leading order coupling

For
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.8
, we find that the largest matrix elements of
{\mathcal{J}}
are
16.38
meV and the second largest matrix elements are
4.28
meV.
We will only keep the largest matrix elements for simplicity.
Due to the constraints derived above, we find the largest elements of
{\mathcal{J}}
are given by

{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 13,\eta 13}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 24,\eta 24}=-{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 13,-\eta 24}=-{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 24,-\eta 13}=J\ .

(S200)

where
J
is the value of the largest matrix element.
The values of
J
for other
w_{0}/w_{1}
are tabulated in
table

S6
.
The interaction Hamiltonian can be simplified as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle-\frac{J}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\big(:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{2}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha+2,\eta s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\alpha+2,\eta s_{1}}:-f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\overline{\alpha},-\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\alpha+2,\eta s_{1}}\big)\ .

(S201)

We can write
{\hat{H}}_{J}
in a more compact form as

H_{J}=-\frac{J}{2N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}(\eta\eta^{\prime}+(-1)^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}):f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}+2,\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\alpha+2,\eta s_{1}}:

(S202)

In
section

S2.7
we have block-diagonalized the flat-U(4) generators (
eqs.

S112
,
S113
and
S114
) such that each block carries a fundamental U(4) representation.
We find that
{\hat{H}}_{J}
is nothing but the coupling between two fundamental flat-U(4) “spins”.
We first define the fundamental flat-U(4) generators at each site (momentum) for
f
-electrons (
c
-electrons) as

\displaystyle{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{ss^{\prime}}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha^{\prime}-1}\eta^{\prime}}\Sigma^{(\mu\nu,f)}_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\ ,

(S203)

\displaystyle{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{ss^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\sum_{aa^{\prime}=1,2}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a^{\prime}-1}\eta^{\prime}}\Sigma^{(\mu\nu,c)}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\ ,

(S204)

\displaystyle{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{ss^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\sum_{aa^{\prime}=3,4}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a^{\prime}-1}\eta^{\prime}}\Sigma^{(\mu\nu,c)}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\ .

(S205)

The
\Sigma^{(\mu\nu,f)}
and
\Sigma^{(\mu\nu,c)}
matrices are given in
eqs.

S106
,
S107
,
S108
and
S109
.

eqs.

S112
,
S113
and
S114
are related to the above equations as
{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})
,

{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime,\xi)}=N{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q}=0)
,

{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}=N{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q}=0)
.
Now we consider the product of flat-U(4) generators
\sum_{\mu\nu\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q})
.
Substituting the explicit forms of the
\Sigma^{(\mu\nu,f)}
and
\Sigma^{(\mu\nu,c)}
matrices into the product and expanding the summation over
\mu
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\nu\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4N}\sum_{\xi}\sum_{\nu=0,x,y,z}\sum_{\eta\alpha}^{\prime}\sum_{\eta^{\prime},a=3,4}^{\prime}\sum_{s_{1}s_{2}s_{3}s_{4}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\bigg(f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}[{\varsigma}_{\nu}]_{s_{1}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a,\eta^{\prime},s_{3}}^{\dagger}[{\varsigma}_{\nu}]_{s_{3}s_{4}}c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta^{\prime},s_{4}}

\displaystyle+\left((-1)^{\alpha+a}-(-1)^{\alpha+a}\eta\eta^{\prime}\right)f_{\mathbf{R},\overline{\alpha},-\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}[{\varsigma}_{\nu}]_{s_{1}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\overline{a},-\eta^{\prime},s_{3}}^{\dagger}[{\varsigma}_{\nu}]_{s_{3}s_{4}}c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta^{\prime},s_{4}}

\displaystyle+\eta\eta^{\prime}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}[{\varsigma}_{\nu}]_{s_{1}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}a\eta^{\prime}s_{3}}^{\dagger}[{\varsigma}_{\nu}]_{s_{3}s_{4}}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta^{\prime}s_{4}}\bigg)\ ,

(S206)

where
\sum_{\eta\alpha}^{\prime}
sums over
\eta,\alpha
satisfying
\xi=(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta
,
\sum_{\eta,a=3,4}^{\prime}
sums over
\eta,a
satisfying
\xi=(-1)^{a-1}\eta
, and
\sum_{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}
sums over
\mathbf{k}
satisfying
|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}
,
|\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}
.
Due to the identity
\sum_{\nu}[{\varsigma}_{\nu}]_{s_{1}s_{2}}[{\varsigma}_{\nu}]_{s_{3}s_{4}}=2\delta_{s_{1}s_{4}}\delta_{s_{2}s_{3}}
, there is

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\nu\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\xi}\sum_{\eta\alpha}^{\prime}\sum_{\eta^{\prime},a=3,4}^{\prime}\sum_{s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\bigg(f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a,\eta^{\prime},s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta^{\prime},s_{1}}+\eta\eta^{\prime}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}a\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta^{\prime}s_{1}}

\displaystyle+\left((-1)^{\alpha+a}-(-1)^{\alpha+a}\eta\eta^{\prime}\right)f_{\mathbf{R},\overline{\alpha},-\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\overline{a},-\eta^{\prime},s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta^{\prime},s_{1}}\bigg)\ .

(S207)

The first line after the equal sign vanishes unless
\eta=\eta^{\prime}
, which, according to
\xi=(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta=(-1)^{a-1}\eta
, also implies
a=\alpha+2
.
The second line after the equal sign vanishes unless
\eta=-\eta^{\prime}
, which, according to
\xi=(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta=(-1)^{a-1}\eta
, also implies
a=\overline{\alpha}+2
.
Therefore, the above equation can be simplified to

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\nu\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\xi}\sum_{\eta\alpha}^{\prime}\sum_{s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}^{\prime}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\bigg(f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta,s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}

\displaystyle-f_{\mathbf{R},\overline{\alpha},-\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{1}}\bigg)\ .

(S208)

The summation
\sum_{\xi}\sum_{\eta\alpha}^{\prime}
can be equivalently replaced by
\sum_{\eta\alpha}
that has no restriction on
\eta\alpha
.
Comparing it with
{\hat{H}}_{J}
, we find
{\hat{H}}_{J}
equals to

{\hat{H}}_{J}=-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\mu\nu\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q}):\ .

(S209)

S3.3.3
Other coupling terms

For
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.8
, we find that the second largest and third largest matrix elements are given by

{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 14,\eta 14}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 23,\eta 23}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 14,-\eta 23}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 14,-\eta 13}=J^{\prime}=\mathrm{4.28meV}\ ,

(S210)

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 12,\eta 23}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 14,\eta 21}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 21,\eta 14}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 23,\eta 12}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 12,-\eta 14}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 14,-\eta 12}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 21,-\eta 23}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 23,-\eta 21}=-J^{\prime\prime}=-\mathrm{1.17meV}\ ,

(S211)

respectively.
Following the calculation around
section

S3.3.2
in last subsubsection, we find that the
J^{\prime}
term can be rewritten as a ferro-magnetic coupling btween
f
-electrons and
c
-electrons

-J^{\prime}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\mu\nu\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,-\xi)}(\mathbf{q}):\ .

(S212)

Different with
J
, which couples U(4) moments of
f
- and
c
-electrons with the same
\xi
index,
J^{\prime}
couples U(4) moments
f
- and
c
-electrons with opposite
\xi
indices.
The
J^{\prime}
term respects the flat-U(4) symmetry because it is a product of flat-U(4) moments, but it breaks the U(4)
\times
U(4) symmetry.

The
J^{\prime\prime}
term can be rewritten as

J^{\prime\prime}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{ss^{\prime}\eta}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\eta 1s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}\eta 2s}+f_{\mathbf{R}-\eta 2s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}-\eta 1s}\right)\left(c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}\eta 3s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\eta 2s^{\prime}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}-\eta 4s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}-\eta 1s^{\prime}}\right)+h.c.

(S213)

J^{\prime\prime}
cannot be written as a product of flat-U(4) moments and it breaks the flat-U(4) symmetry.
To see the symmetry breaking, we consider to apply the valley rotation
e^{-i\frac{\pi}{2}\hat{\Sigma}_{x0}}
(
eq.

S105
).
Using the explicit U(4) representations defined in
eqs.

S106
,
S107
,
S108
and
S109
, we find that the
J^{\prime\prime}
term transforms to

\to-J^{\prime\prime}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{ss^{\prime}\eta}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\eta 1s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}\eta 2s}+f_{\mathbf{R}-\eta 2s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}-\eta 1s}\right)\left(c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}\eta 3s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\eta 2s^{\prime}}+c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}-\eta 4s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}-\eta 1s^{\prime}}\right)+h.c.

(S214)

Thus the
J^{\prime\prime}
term is odd under
e^{-i\frac{\pi}{2}\hat{\Sigma}_{x0}}
.
Since
J^{\prime\prime}=-1.17
meV is small compared to other energy scales,
e.g.
,
U_{1}=57.95
meV,
J=16.38
meV, flat-U(4) is still an approximate symmetry of the full interaction Hamiltonian.

It was shown in Ref.
Bernevig et al. 2021a
that in the flat nonchiral limit the flat-U(4) is an exact symmetry of the projected Coulomb Hamiltonian.
But here we find that the flat-U(4) symmetry is broken by
J^{\prime\prime}
.
The reason for this seemingly contradiction is that the
\Gamma_{3}
states, which are absent in the projected Coulomb Hamiltonian, are added in the heavy fermion model.
Notice that the inter-valley rotation in Ref.
Bernevig et al. 2021a
is generated by the unitary operator
C_{2z}P
, while the inter-valley rotation here is generated by
C_{2z}PS
, where
S
is the third-chiral operator defined in
section

S2.7
.

S
is needed in our model because otherwise the inter-valley rotation would not commute with the kinetic energy of the dispersive
c
-bands.
The representations of the two operators are given by
D^{f}(C_{2z}P)=-i\sigma_{y}\tau_{y}
,
D^{c}(C_{2z}P)=(i\sigma_{y}\tau_{y})\oplus(i\sigma_{y}\tau_{y})
and
D^{f}(C_{2z}PS)=-i\sigma_{y}\tau_{y}
,
D^{c}(C_{2z}PS)=(-i\sigma_{y}\tau_{y})\oplus(i\sigma_{y}\tau_{y})
, respectively, where the first and second blocks of
D^{c}
act on
\Gamma_{3}
and
\Gamma_{1}\oplus\Gamma_{2}
states, respectively.
Since
J
and
J^{\prime}
terms only involve
f
-electrons and
\Gamma_{1}\oplus\Gamma_{2}

c
-electrons, on which
C_{2z}P
and
C_{2z}PS
actions are the same, the conclusion of Ref.
Bernevig et al. 2021a
applies and hence
J
,
J^{\prime}
terms respect the flat-U(4) symmetry.
However the
J^{\prime\prime}
term involves
\Gamma_{3}

c
-electrons, on which
C_{2z}P
and
C_{2z}PS
actions are different, hence the conclusion of Ref.
Bernevig et al. 2021a
does not apply to
J^{\prime\prime}
.

S3.4
Double hybridization terms (the third and fourth terms)

Here we determine the double hybridization terms,
i.e.
, the third and fourth terms discussed in
section

S3.1
.
We first consider the
:\rho_{fc}:V:\rho_{fc}:
term

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}\\
\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R}_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1}\\
\mathbf{R}_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}w^{(\eta_{1})*}_{l_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})e^{i\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{1}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{i\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}_{1}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}

(S215)

As explained at the beginning of
section

S3.3
, since the Wannier functions are sharp Gaussian functions and
V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})
decays quickly as
|\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2}|
grows, the largest contribution to the above equation should come from the
\mathbf{R}_{1}=\mathbf{R}_{2}
terms.
Therefore, in the following we only focus on the
\mathbf{R}_{1}=\mathbf{R}_{2}
term.
Substituting the Fourier transformation of the interaction, we can rewrite the interaction as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}}\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{q})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}\\
\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\\
a_{1}a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}w^{(\eta_{1})*}_{l_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}

\displaystyle\times w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}e^{i(\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}+\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}})\cdot\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}

(S216)

According to
eq.

S45
, there is

\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}w^{(\eta_{1})*}_{l_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{q})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}=\sqrt{\Omega_{0}}\ e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{q})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{q})\ .

(S217)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}V(\mathbf{q})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}\\
\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\\
a_{1}a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}\\
\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}\end{subarray}}\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{q})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})

\displaystyle\times e^{i(\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}+\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{q}+\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}+\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}+\mathbf{q})\cdot\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\ .

(S218)

Since
\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}
is always a moiré reciprocal lattice, there must be
e^{i(\eta_{1}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{1}}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}-\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}=1
.
We can further simplify the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}V(\mathbf{q})\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\\
a_{1}a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\rangle\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})\rangle

\displaystyle\times e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\ .

(S219)

We define the matrix

X_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})=\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}V(\mathbf{q})\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\alpha_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\rangle\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{2})\rangle

(S220)

such that the interaction can be written as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\\
a_{1}a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}X_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\ .

(S221)

For low energy states around
\mathbf{k}=0
, we can approximate
X_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{2})
by
X_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}(0,0)
(dubbed as
\widetilde{J}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}
)

\widetilde{J}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}=\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}V(\mathbf{q})\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\alpha_{1}}(-\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(0)\rangle\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(0)\rangle

(S222)

We find that, according to the time-reversal symmetry, the matrix elements of
\widetilde{J}
are same as those of
{\mathcal{J}}
(
eq.

S189
).
The
T
symmetry (
eq.

S16
) and its representations (
eqs.

S36
and
S2.2
) imply
\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{k})=\widetilde{v}^{(-\eta_{2})*}_{-\mathbf{Q}\beta,\alpha_{2}}(-\mathbf{k})
,
\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a_{2}}(0)=\widetilde{u}^{(-\eta_{2})*}_{-\mathbf{Q}\beta,a_{2}}(0)
and hence

\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}}(\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(0)\rangle=\langle\widetilde{u}^{(-\eta_{2})}_{{a}_{2}}(0)|\widetilde{v}^{(-\eta_{2})}_{{\alpha}_{2}}(-\mathbf{q})\rangle\ .

(S223)

Thus we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha_{2}a_{2}}=\int\frac{d^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}V(\mathbf{q})\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\alpha_{1}}(-\mathbf{q})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(0)\rangle\langle\widetilde{u}^{(-\eta_{2})}_{{a}_{2}}(0)|\widetilde{v}^{(-\eta_{2})}_{{\alpha}_{2}}(-\mathbf{q})\rangle={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},-\eta_{2}{\alpha}_{2}{a}_{2}}\ .

(S224)

Thus
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}
can be written as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\\
a_{1}a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta_{1}\alpha_{1}a_{1},\eta_{2}\overline{\alpha}_{2}\overline{a}_{2}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\ .

(S225)

According to
eq.

S200
, the non-negligible matrix elements of
{\mathcal{J}}
and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{J}}}
are given by

{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 13,\eta 13}={\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 24,\eta 24}=-{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 13,-\eta 24}=-{\mathcal{J}}_{\eta 24,-\eta 13}=J\ ,

(S226)

\widetilde{{\mathcal{J}}}_{\eta 13,-\eta 13}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{J}}}_{\eta 24,-\eta 24}=-\widetilde{{\mathcal{J}}}_{\eta 13,\eta 24}=-\widetilde{{\mathcal{J}}}_{\eta 24,\eta 13}=J\ .

(S227)

The interaction Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}
can be explicitly written as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=

\displaystyle-\frac{J}{2N}\sum_{\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\left(f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\overline{\alpha}\eta s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\overline{\alpha}+2,\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\alpha+2,\eta s_{1}}-f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha,-\eta,s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha+2,-\eta,s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\alpha+2,\eta s_{1}}\right)\ .

(S228)

We can write
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}
in a more compact form as

{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=-\frac{J}{4N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}(\eta\eta^{\prime}-(-1)^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}})f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}+2,\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\alpha+2,\eta s_{1}}

(S229)

The fourth term in
section

S3.1
,
i.e.
,
:\rho_{cf}:V:\rho_{cf}:
, is just the hermitian conjugation of
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}
.
Thus the total double hybridization interaction is given by

{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}={\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}+{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}^{\dagger}\ .

(S230)

S3.5
Density-hybridization terms (the sixth, seventh, eighth, and ninth terms)

S3.5.1
Vanishing sixth and seventh terms

We now study the sixth and seventh terms discussed in
section

S3.1
.
The sixth term (
:\hat{\rho}_{ff}:V:\hat{\rho}_{fc}:+(\leftrightarrow)
) has the form

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{67}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\Omega_{\rm tot}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}n_{f}(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{R})\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}\alpha^{\prime}a}\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(\mathbf{k})e^{i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}e^{i\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l}\cdot\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle\times\left(:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}+f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\right)\ .

(S231)

We have omitted the overlap of local orbitals at different sites.
Changing the variables
\mathbf{r}_{1}\to\mathbf{r}_{1}+\mathbf{R}
,
\mathbf{r}_{2}\to\mathbf{r}_{2}+\mathbf{R}
, we can rewrite the interaction as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{67}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\Omega_{\rm tot}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}n_{f}(\mathbf{r}_{1})\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}\alpha^{\prime}a}\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r}_{2})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(\mathbf{k})e^{i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle\times\left(:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}+f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\right)\ .

(S232)

We have made use of the fact that
\eta\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l}-\mathbf{Q}
is a moiré reciprocal lattice and
e^{i(\eta\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot\mathbf{R}}=1
.
We introduce the matrix

\displaystyle X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(\mathbf{k})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Omega_{0}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})n_{f}(\mathbf{r}_{1})\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r}_{2})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(\mathbf{k})e^{i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{Q})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}

(S233)

such that the interaction can be written as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{67}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}\alpha^{\prime}a}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}X^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha^{\prime},a}(\mathbf{k})e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\left(:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}+f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\right)\ .

(S234)

Since only the conduction states around
\mathbf{k}=0
are relevant in the low energy physics, we will approximate
X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(\mathbf{k})
by
X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)

\displaystyle X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Omega_{0}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})n_{f}(\mathbf{r}_{1})\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r}_{2})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)e^{-i\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}\ .

(S235)

Now we show that
X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)
is guaranteed to be vanishing by symmetries.
According to the
C_{3z}
symmetry (
eqs.

S16
and
S23
), there is

e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta_{2}[\sigma_{z}]_{\beta\beta}}w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r}_{2})=w^{(\eta_{2})}_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}(C_{3z}\mathbf{r}_{2})\zeta_{\eta_{2}\alpha}^{f},\qquad e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta_{2}[\sigma_{z}]_{\beta\beta}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)=\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{C_{3z}\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)\zeta^{c}_{\eta_{2}a}

(S236)

with
\zeta_{\eta\alpha}^{f}
and
\zeta_{\eta\alpha}^{c}
being
C_{3z}
eigenvalues given in
eq.

S36
and
section

S2.2
, respectively,
i.e.
,

\zeta_{\eta_{2}\alpha}^{f}=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta_{2}[\sigma_{z}]_{\alpha\alpha}},\qquad\zeta_{\eta_{2}\alpha}^{c}=\begin{cases}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\eta_{2}[\sigma_{z}]_{aa}},\qquad&a=1,2\\
1,\qquad&a=3,4\end{cases}\ .

(S237)

Substituting this condition into
eq.

S235
, we have

\displaystyle X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)=

\displaystyle\zeta_{\eta_{2},\alpha^{\prime}}^{f*}\zeta_{\eta_{2},a}^{c}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Omega_{0}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})n_{f}(\mathbf{r}_{1})\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}(C_{3z}\mathbf{r}_{2})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{C_{3z}\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)e^{-i\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}

(S238)

Changing the variables as
\mathbf{r}_{1}\to C_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{r}_{1}
,
\mathbf{r}_{2}\to C_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{r}_{2}
,
\mathbf{Q}\to C_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{Q}
, we have

\displaystyle X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)=

\displaystyle\zeta_{\eta_{2},\alpha^{\prime}}^{f*}\zeta_{\eta_{2},a}^{c}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Omega_{0}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(C_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{r}_{1}-C_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{r}_{2})n_{f}(C_{3z}^{-1}\mathbf{r}_{1})\sum_{l\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l\eta_{2}}}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l\beta,\alpha^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r}_{2})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}\beta,a}(0)e^{-i\mathbf{Q}\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}

(S239)

Making use of
V(C_{3z}\mathbf{r})=V(\mathbf{r})
,
n_{f}(C_{3z}\mathbf{r})=n_{f}(\mathbf{r})
, we have

X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)=\zeta_{\eta_{2},\alpha^{\prime}}^{f*}\zeta_{\eta_{2},a}^{c}X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)\ .

(S240)

Therefore,
X_{\alpha^{\prime},a}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)
is zero if
\zeta_{\eta_{2},\alpha^{\prime}}^{f}\neq\zeta_{\eta_{2},a}^{c}
.
Due to the
C_{3z}
eigenvalues the only possible nonzero matrix elements are
X_{1,1}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)
and
X_{2,2}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)
.
The above symmetry analysis also applies for other symmetries, for example the particle-hole symmetry
P
.
As explained in the end of
section

S2.2
, the
a=1,2
states have opposite
P
eigenvalues with the
\alpha=1,2
states.
Thus
X_{1,1}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)
and
X_{2,2}^{(\eta_{2})}(0)
are also guaranteed to be zero.
Thus the matrix elements in
{\hat{H}}_{67}
vanish to zeroth order of
\mathbf{k}
.

Since the seventh term discussed in
section

S3.1
is the hermitian conjugation of the sixth term, its matrix elements also vanish to zeroth order of
\mathbf{k}
.
The sixth and seventh interaction terms will be non-vanishing if we include higher order terms of
\mathbf{k}
in the matrix elements,
i.e.
, the
\mathbf{k}
-dependence in
\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{\mathbf{Q}\alpha,a}(\mathbf{k})
.
But these matrix elements will be small for low energy conduction band states around
\mathbf{k}=0
.
We claim that these
\mathbf{k}
-depend matrix elements in the density-hybridization interaction are irrelevant in the low energy physics.

S3.5.2
The eighth and ninth terms

We now study the eighth and ninth terms discussed in
section

S3.1
.
They are weak because the hybridization,
i.e.
,
\langle f^{\dagger}c\rangle
and
\langle c^{\dagger}f\rangle
, is small, as will be discussed in
section

S4
.
The eighth term (
:\hat{\rho}_{cc}:V:\hat{\rho}_{fc}:+(\leftrightarrow)
) has the form

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{K+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2})\sum_{\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{1},\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{1}}\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\beta_{1},a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a}(\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}e^{i\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle\times\left(:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\right)\ .

(S241)

Applying the Fourier transformation of
V(\mathbf{r})
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{K+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Omega_{\rm tot}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}\sum_{\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{1},\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}}V(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\beta_{1},a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a}(\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}e^{i\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle\times\left(:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\right)\ .

(S242)

According to
eq.

S45
, there are

\displaystyle\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}w^{(\eta_{2})*}_{l_{2}\beta_{2},\alpha}(\mathbf{r}_{2}-\mathbf{R})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{2}}=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\Omega_{0}}\ e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\beta_{2},\alpha}(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})\ .

(S243)

Since
\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}
is a moiré reciprocal lattice, it must be that
e^{i(\eta_{2}\Delta\mathbf{K}_{l_{2}}-\mathbf{Q}_{2}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}=1
.
We hence obtain

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{K+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\beta_{1}l_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{1},\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}}V(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\beta_{1},a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\beta_{2}l_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
a_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}}\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{2};\alpha}(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a}(\mathbf{k}_{2})e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle\times\left(:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\right)\ .

(S244)

We define the matrix

\displaystyle X_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{2})=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}\beta_{1}l_{2}\beta_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{1},\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}}V(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\times\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{1})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\beta_{1},a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{2};\alpha}(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a}(\mathbf{k}_{2})

(S245)

such that the interaction can be written as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{K+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}\\
a_{2}\end{subarray}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}X_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{2})

\displaystyle\times\left(:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\right)\ .

(S246)

Since the conduction bands only have low energy states around
\mathbf{k}=0
, thus in the following we approximate
X_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}(\mathbf{k}_{1},\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}_{2})
by
X_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}(0,0,0)
, dubbed as
{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}\beta_{1}l_{2}\beta_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{1},\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}}V(-\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime}\beta_{1},a_{1}^{\prime}}(0)\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}+\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\prime},\beta_{2};\alpha}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{l_{1}\beta_{1}l_{2}\beta_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{1}\eta_{1}}}\sum_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\in\mathcal{Q}_{l_{2}\eta_{2}}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}V(\mathbf{G})\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}\beta_{1},a_{1}}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{1}-\mathbf{G},\beta_{1};a_{1}^{\prime}}(0)\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})*}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}-\mathbf{G},\beta_{2};\alpha}(0)\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\mathbf{Q}_{2}\beta_{2},a}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{G}}V(\mathbf{G})\langle\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}}(0)|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{1})}_{a_{1}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{G})\rangle\langle\widetilde{v}^{(\eta_{2})}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{G})|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta_{2})}_{a_{2}}(0)\rangle\ .

(S247)

The Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{K+}
can be written in a simple form

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{K+}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime}a_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}-\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}+f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}a_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}s_{1}}:\right)\ .

(S248)

We now study the
{\mathcal{K}}
matrix.
According to the
C_{2z}P
symmetry (
eqs.

S36
and
S65
), there are

C_{2z}P|\widetilde{v}^{(\eta)}_{\alpha}(\mathbf{k})\rangle=-e^{i\pi\alpha}e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}\eta}|\widetilde{v}^{(-\eta)}_{\overline{\alpha}}(\mathbf{k})\rangle,\qquad C_{2z}P|\widetilde{u}^{(\eta)}_{a}(\mathbf{k})\rangle=e^{i\pi a}e^{i\frac{\pi}{2}\eta}|\widetilde{v}^{(-\eta)}_{\overline{a}}(\mathbf{k})\rangle,

(S249)

where
\overline{\alpha}=2,1
for
\alpha=1,2
and
\overline{a}=2,1,4,3
for
a=1,2,3,4
.
Then if follows that the
{\mathcal{K}}
matrix satisfies

{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}=e^{i\pi(a_{1}^{\prime}-a_{1})}{\mathcal{K}}_{-\eta_{1}\overline{a}_{1}\overline{a}_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}=-e^{i\pi(a_{2}-\alpha)}{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},-\eta_{2}\overline{\alpha}\overline{a}_{2}}

(S250)

Similarly, due to the
C_{2x}
symmetry (
eqs.

S36
and
S2.2
), the
{\mathcal{K}}
matrix satisfies

{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}={\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}\overline{a}_{1}\overline{a}_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\overline{\alpha}\overline{a}_{2}}\ .

(S251)

Due to the
C_{2z}TP
symmetry (
eqs.

S36
and
S65
), the
{\mathcal{K}}
matrix satisfies

{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}a_{1}a_{1}^{\prime},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}=(-1)^{a_{1}+a_{1}^{\prime}}{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}\overline{a}_{1}^{\prime}\overline{a}_{1},\eta_{2}\alpha a_{2}}\ .

(S252)

Numerically we find that there is only one independent non-negligible matrix element

{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}14,\eta_{2}24}={\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}23,\eta_{2}13}=-{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}32,\eta_{2}24}=-{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta_{1}41,\eta_{2}13}=\eta_{1}\eta_{2}K\ ,

(S253)

where
K=4.887
meV for
w_{0}/w_{1}=0.8
.
The second largest matrix element is
0.82
meV.
One can verify that the
{\mathcal{K}}
matrix satisfies the constraints derived above.
Therefore, we can write the Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{K+}
as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{K+}=

\displaystyle\frac{K}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bigg(c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}\overline{\alpha}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\alpha+2,\eta_{1}s_{1}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha+2,\eta_{2}s_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha+2,\eta_{2}s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\overline{\alpha}+2,\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\alpha,\eta_{1}s_{1}}\bigg)\ .

(S254)

The ninth term discussed in
section

S3.1
,
i.e.
,
:\hat{\rho}_{cc}:V:\hat{\rho}_{cf}:+(\leftrightarrow)
, is just the hermitian conjugation of
{\hat{H}}_{K+}
.
Thus the total density-hybridization Hamiltonian is given by

{\hat{H}}_{K}={\hat{H}}_{K+}+{\hat{H}}_{K+}^{\dagger}\ .

(S255)

S3.6
Summary of the interaction Hamiltonian and its symmetries

S3.6.1
Summary of the interaction Hamiltonian

In summary, the interaction Hamiltonian is given by

{\hat{H}}_{I}={\hat{H}}_{U}+{\hat{H}}_{V}+{\hat{H}}_{W}+{\hat{H}}_{J}+{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}+{\hat{H}}_{K}\ .

(S256)

{\hat{H}}_{U}
(
eq.

S157
) is the density-density interaction of the local orbitals

{\hat{H}}_{U}=\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}::f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:+\frac{U_{2}}{2}\sum_{\langle\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\rangle}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}::f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:\ ,

(S257)

where
\langle\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\rangle
indexes nearest neighbor pairs on the triangular lattice,
U_{1}
is the on-site repulsion interaction, and
U_{2}
is the repulsion between nearest neighbors.

{\hat{H}}_{V}
(
eq.

S179
) is the density-density interaction of the conduction bands

{\hat{H}}_{V}=\frac{1}{2\Omega_{0}N}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}a_{1}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}a_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{q}\\
|\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda_{c}\end{subarray}}V(\mathbf{q}):c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{q}a_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{q}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2}a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}:\ ,

(S258)

where
V(\mathbf{q})
(
eq.

S145
) is the Fourier transformation of the double-gate-screened Coulomb interaction.

{\hat{H}}_{W}
(
eq.
