S172
) is the density-density interaction between the local orbitals and the conduction bands

{\hat{H}}_{W}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}a}W_{a}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\cdot\mathbf{R}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}::c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}:\ ,

(S259)

where
W_{1}=W_{2}
and
W_{3}=W_{4}
due to symmetries.

{\hat{H}}_{J}
(
eq.

S202
) is the exchange interaction between local orbitals and the conduction bands

\displaystyle H_{J}=-\frac{J}{2N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}-\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}(\eta\eta^{\prime}+(-1)^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}}):f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}::c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}+2,\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\alpha+2,\eta s_{1}}:\ .

(S260)

{\hat{H}}_{J}
can be equivalently written as ferromagnetic coupling between the flat-U(4) momenta (
eq.

S209
)

{\hat{H}}_{J}=-J\sum_{\mu\nu\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q})\ ,

(S261)

with the momenta given by
eqs.

S203
and
S205
.

{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}={\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}+{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}^{\dagger}
is the double-hybridization interaction, with
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}
(
eq.

S229
) given by

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}+}=-\frac{J}{4N}\sum_{\mathbf{R}s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}\eta\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{1}+\mathbf{k}_{2})\cdot\mathbf{R}}(\eta\eta^{\prime}-(-1)^{\alpha+\alpha^{\prime}})f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}+2,\eta^{\prime}s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\alpha+2,\eta s_{1}}\ .

(S262)

Notice that the coefficients
J
in
{\hat{H}}_{J}
and
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}
are the same coefficient because the corresponding matrix elements are related by
T
symmetry, as explained in
section

S3.4
.

{\hat{H}}_{K}={\hat{H}}_{K+}+{\hat{H}}_{K+}^{\dagger}
is the density-hybridization interaction, with
{\hat{H}}_{K+}
(
section

S3.5.2
) given by

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{K+}=

\displaystyle\frac{K}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}\Omega_{0}}\sum_{\eta_{1}s_{1}\eta_{2}s_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}_{1}|,|\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}|,|\mathbf{k}_{2}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha}e^{i(\mathbf{k}_{2}+\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{R}}\eta_{1}\eta_{2}\bigg(c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}\overline{\alpha}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\alpha+2,\eta_{1}s_{1}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha+2,\eta_{2}s_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt-f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{2},\alpha+2,\eta_{2}s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1},\overline{\alpha}+2,\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}_{1}^{\prime},\alpha,\eta_{1}s_{1}}\bigg)\ .

(S263)

The parameters
U_{1,2}
,
W_{1,3}
,
J
,
K
for different
w_{0}/w_{1}
are given in
table

S6
.

S3.6.2
Symmetries of the interaction Hamiltonian

The continuous BM model (with interaction) has a U(2)
\times
U(2) symmetry when valley is a good quantum number.
Thus the effective topological heavy fermion model must also have a U(2)
\times
U(2) symmetry.
As discussed in
section

S2.7
, when
M=0
, the presence of the
C_{2z}PS
symmetry, where
S
is the third chiral symmetry, will enhance the U(2)
\times
U(2) symmetry to a U(4) symmetry, referred to as the flat U(4) symmetry.
Recall that the Coulomb interaction is given by (
eq.

S137
)

\frac{1}{2}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2}):\hat{\rho}(\mathbf{r}_{1})::\hat{\rho}(\mathbf{r}_{2}):

(S264)

with
:\hat{\rho}(\mathbf{r}_{1}):
being the normal ordered projected density operator (
section

S3.1
).
Since
C_{2z}P
(
eqs.

S36
and
S65
) is a symmetry of the original model,
:\hat{\rho}(\mathbf{r}):
and hence
{\hat{H}}_{I}
commute with
C_{2z}P
.
(In Ref.
Bernevig et al. 2021a
, it is
C_{2z}P
that gives the U(4) symmetry, provided that remote bands are projected out and the kinetic energy of the flat bands is neglected.)
Thus, in order for
H_{I}
to have the flat-U(4) symmetry, we only need
H_{I}
to commute with
S
defined in
eq.

S100
.
One can easily verify that
{\hat{H}}_{U}
,
{\hat{H}}_{V}
,
{\hat{H}}_{W}
,
{\hat{H}}_{J}
,
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}
commute with
S
whereas
{\hat{H}}_{K}
anti-commutes with
S
.
Therefore,
{\hat{H}}_{U}
,
{\hat{H}}_{V}
,
{\hat{H}}_{W}
,
{\hat{H}}_{J}
,
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}
respect the flat-U(4) symmetry while
{\hat{H}}_{K}
breaks the flat-U(4) symmetry.
(The neglected terms
J^{\prime}
and
J^{\prime\prime}
discussed in
section

S3.3.3
commute and anti-commute with
S
, respectively. Thus
J^{\prime}
respects the flat-U(4) symmetry whereas
J^{\prime\prime}
breaks the flat-U(4) symmetries.)
Since
K
(and
J^{\prime\prime}
) is small compared to other interaction parameters (
table

S6
), flat-U(4) is still an approximate symmetry of the interaction Hamiltonian.
(Including the omitted small matrix elements in the interaction Hamiltonian and the
\mathbf{k}
-dependence of these matrix elements may yield more terms that break the flat-U(4).
However, we claim these effects are weak in the low energy physics.)

For the same reason as the flat-U(4) symmetry,
H_{I}
will have the chiral-U(4) symmetry as long as it commutes with
C
defined in
eq.

S86
.
(The free part
{\hat{H}}_{0}
anti-commute with
C
when
v_{\star}^{\prime}=0
.)
One can easily verify that
{\hat{H}}_{U}
,
{\hat{H}}_{V}
,
{\hat{H}}_{W}
,
{\hat{H}}_{J}
,
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}
, and
{\hat{H}}_{K}
all commute with
C
.
Therefore, the chiral-U(4) symmetry is an exact symmetry of the interaction Hamiltonian summarized in
section

S3.6.1
.
Since for nonzero
w_{0}/w_{1}
, the continuous model does not really respect the first chiral symmetry, we expect that including the omitted small terms in
{\hat{H}}_{I}
will break the chiral symmetry and hence the chiral-U(4).
Nevertheless, chiral-U(4) can be thought as an approximate symmetry of
{\hat{H}}_{I}
.

As discussed in
section

S2.9
, the presence of both flat-U(4) and chiral-U(4) implies an SU(4)
\times
SU(4) symmetry.
Given that the charge-U(1) is respected, the SU(4)
\times
SU(4) group will be promoted to U(4)
\times
U(4) if the Hamiltonian further conserves the index
\xi
(
\xi=\eta(-1)^{\alpha-1}
for
f
-electrons and
\xi=\eta(-1)^{a-1}
for
c
-electrons).
Since
{\hat{H}}_{U}
,
{\hat{H}}_{V}
,
{\hat{H}}_{W}
,
{\hat{H}}_{J}
,
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}
all have both flat-U(4) and chiral-U(4) symmetries and
\xi
is repsected in all of them, they all have the U(4)
\times
U(4) symmetry.
However, as discussed in the last two paragraphs
{\hat{H}}_{K}
only has the chiral-U(4) symmetry.
In
table

S5
we summarize the local symmetries of all the single-particle and interaction Hamiltonian terms.

In
eq.

S84
we defined the charge-conjugation symmetry

{\mathcal{P}}_{c}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}_{c}^{-1}=\sum_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}D^{f}_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}(PC_{2z}T)f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s},\qquad{\mathcal{P}}_{c}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}{\mathcal{P}}_{c}^{-1}=\sum_{a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}D^{c}_{a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},a\eta}(PC_{2z}T)c_{-\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s}\ ,

(S265)

where

D^{f}(C_{2z}TP)=-\sigma_{y}\tau_{z},\qquad D^{f}(C_{2z}TP)=(\sigma_{y}\tau_{z})\oplus(\sigma_{y}\tau_{z})\ ,

(S266)

and showed that the single-particle Hamiltonian (
section

S2.5
) respects the charge conjugation symmetry.
One can show
{\mathcal{P}}_{c}:\hat{\rho}(\mathbf{r}):{\mathcal{P}}_{c}^{-1}=-:\hat{\rho}(\mathbf{r}):
, which automatically implies that the Coulomb interaction of the form

\frac{1}{2}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{1}\int d^{2}\mathbf{r}_{2}V(\mathbf{r}_{1}-\mathbf{r}_{2}):\hat{\rho}(\mathbf{r}_{1})::\hat{\rho}(\mathbf{r}_{2}):

(S267)

is invariant under the charge conjugation.

S3.7
Failure of the strong coupling picture in the first chiral limit of the BM model (
w_{0}=0
)

The first chiral limit discussed in
section

S2.6
is achieved by artificially enforcing
v_{\star}^{\prime}=0
in the single-particle Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{0}
, which is obtained from the BM model with nonzero
w_{0}
.
In the actual chiral limit of the BM model, where
w_{0}=0
, the parameter
v_{\star}^{\prime}
automatically vanishes.
In this subsection we discuss the heavy fermion model in this actual chiral limit (
w_{0}=0
).
We construct the maximally localized Wannier functions by the same procedure as in
section

S2.1
.
Since for
w_{0}=0
the
\Gamma_{3}
states have energies about
\pm
100meV, the previously used Wannierization energy window [
-80
meV,
80
meV] now do not include the
\Gamma_{3}
states and hence do not support local Wannier functions.
We hence change the energy window to [
-120
meV,
120
meV] and use all the same other parameters (given around
eq.

S42
) for the Wannierization.
The resulted Wannier functions still decay exponentially but are much less localized.
The on-site repulsion
U_{1}
of
f
-electrons becomes extremely small (
<1
meV) while the hybridization
\gamma
between
f
- and
c
-electrons becomes quite strong (
\sim
100meV).
The property
U_{1}\ll\gamma
does not change if we use a larger Wannierization energy window,
e.g.
, [
-160
meV,
160
meV].
Therefore, in the actual first chiral limit, the strong coupling picture (
U_{1}>\gamma
) is no longer valid, and the quantum-dot-like behaviors observed in STMs
Xie et al. 2019
;
Wong et al. 2020
cannot be easily explained.

Nevertheless, from a theoretical perspective, as explained in
section

S2.10
, our model have the key geometric features of the BM model in the chiral limit: The flat bands are analytically solvable, and they satisfy the ideal droplet condition and have relatively flat Berry’s curvature.

S4
Correlated insulator states

S4.1
Mean field Hamiltonian

{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}
will create two particles (or holes) in the local orbitals, which will cost an energy of the order
U_{1}
.
Since
U_{1}
is the largest energy scale of the problem,
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}
is an high-energy process and will be omitted in the following.
From a mean field aspect, the energy contributed by the
{\hat{H}}_{\widetilde{J}}
term is
\sim J|\langle f^{\dagger}c\rangle|^{2}
.
Since
U_{1}
is much larger than the coupling between
f
- and
c
-electrons,
\langle f^{\dagger}c\rangle
can be treated as a small quantity and its second order terms,
e.g.
,
J|\langle f^{\dagger}c\rangle|^{2}
can be omitted.
We will also omit
{\hat{H}}_{K}
for a similar reason.
From the mean field aspect, the energy contributed by
{\hat{H}}_{K}
is
\sim K\langle f^{\dagger}c\rangle\langle c^{\dagger}c\rangle
.
According to
section

S3.5.2
, the involved
\langle c^{\dagger}c\rangle
is off-diagonal in the
a
-index,
e.g.
,
\langle c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}\overline{\alpha}\eta s}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha+2,\eta s}\rangle
, which must be small because only diagonal terms are non-vanishing in the normal state.
Thus
\sim K\langle f^{\dagger}c\rangle\langle c^{\dagger}c\rangle
can also be treated as a second order small quantity.
Therefore, in the following we approximate the interaction Hamiltonian as
{\hat{H}}_{I}\approx{\hat{H}}_{U}+{\hat{H}}_{W}+{\hat{H}}_{V}+{\hat{H}}_{J}
.

In the following we apply Hartree-Fock (HF) calculation to the remaining terms.
Let us denote the variational slater determinant state as
|\Psi\rangle
.

|\Psi\rangle
is supposed to minimize the total energy
\langle\Psi|{\hat{H}}_{0}+{\hat{H}}_{I}|\Psi\rangle
.
To simplify the calculation, we rewrite the operator
{\hat{H}}_{I}
as
{\hat{H}}_{I}=:{\hat{H}}_{I}:+{\hat{H}}_{I,MF}
, where
:{\hat{H}}_{I}:
is the normal ordered operator of
{\hat{H}}
with respect to
|\Psi\rangle
(not
|G_{0}\rangle
) and
{\hat{H}}_{I,MF}
is
defined
as
{\hat{H}}_{I}-:{\hat{H}}_{I}:
.

:{\hat{H}}_{I}:
by definition will either annihilate
|\Psi\rangle
or excite two particles plus two holes upon
|\Psi\rangle
.
Then it follows that
\langle\Psi|{\hat{H}}_{0}+{\hat{H}}_{I}|\Psi\rangle=\langle\Psi|{\hat{H}}_{0}+{\hat{H}}_{I,MF}|\Psi\rangle
.
Therefore, one only need to minimize
\langle\Psi|{\hat{H}}_{0}+{\hat{H}}_{I,MF}|\Psi\rangle
.

In this subsection we explicitly derive
{\hat{H}}_{I,MF}
.
For simplicity, we assume that the ground state (
|\Psi\rangle
) preserves the translation symmetry.
For later convenience, we introduce the density matrices

O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}=\langle\Psi|f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}|\Psi\rangle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in{\rm MBZ}}\langle\Psi|f_{\mathbf{k}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{k}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}|\Psi\rangle\ ,

(S268)

O^{c}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\left(\langle\Psi|c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}|\Psi\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{aa^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}\right)

(S269)

O^{cf}_{a\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}e^{-i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\langle\Psi|c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}|\Psi\rangle\ .

(S270)

We have defined
O^{c}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}
as the expectation of the conduction band density operator with respect to the charge neutrality point such that it will remain finite even we take the cutoff
\Lambda_{c}
to infinity.
The filling of local orbitals and conduction bands are given by

\nu_{f}=\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}]-4,\qquad\nu_{c}=\mathrm{Tr}[O^{c}]\ .

(S271)

We also assume that there is no pairing in
|\Psi\rangle
.
The total filling with respect to the charge neutrality point is given by
\nu=\nu_{f}+\nu_{c}
.
For the normal state
|G_{0}\rangle
(defined in
section

S3.1
) at charge neutrality point, there is
\nu=\nu_{f}=\nu_{c}=0
.

We first decouple the density-density interactions of local orbitals,
i.e.
,
{\hat{H}}_{U}
in
eq.

S256
.
To derive the mean field Hamiltonian, we first divide the density-density interaction into constant, bilinear, and quartic terms as

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{U}=

\displaystyle 8NU_{1}+48NU_{2}-(4U_{1}+24U_{2})\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\eta s\\
\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\frac{U_{2}}{2}\sum_{\langle\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\rangle}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\eta s\\
\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 8NU_{1}+48NU_{2}-(3.5U_{1}+24U_{2})\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}+\frac{U_{1}}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\eta s\\
\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}

\displaystyle+\frac{U_{2}}{2}\sum_{\langle\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\rangle}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\eta s\\
\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}

(S272)

The mean field Hamiltonian is obtained by projecting out the terms that create two particles and two holes upon the ground state
|\Psi\rangle
.
According to the Wick’s theorem we have

\displaystyle f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}=:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}:+\hbox to5.47pt{\vbox to8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.73436pt\lower-4.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

(S273)

Here
:A:
is the normal ordered form of
A
with respect to
|\Psi\rangle
(
not

|G_{0}\rangle
defined in
section

S3.1
).
The first term will either annihilate
|\Psi\rangle
or excite two particle-hole pairs on top of
|\Psi\rangle
.
We will omit the first term and approximate the four fermion operator by bilinear terms and constant terms

{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.73436pt}{-2.25pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.73436pt}{-2.25pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\hbox to5.47pt{\vbox to8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.73436pt\lower-4.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.73436pt}{-2.25pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 0 13.49 L 0 13.49 L 0 9.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\hbox to5.47pt{\vbox to8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.73436pt\lower-4.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.73436pt}{-2.25pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 0 17.64 L 0 17.64 L 0 9.69}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}\vbox to15.24994pt{}{}\ .

(S275)

Since the on-site repulsion
U_{1}
is the largest energy scale and dominates the correlation physics, we
assume
that only on-site order parameters,
i.e.
,
\langle f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\rangle
, are formed.
Then we can write the approximate on-site and off-site quartic terms as

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\eta s\\
\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}\approx

\displaystyle 2\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}]f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}-2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\eta s\\
\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s,\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}-\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}]\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}]+\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}O^{f}]

(S276)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\eta s\\
\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}\end{subarray}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}]f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}+\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}]f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}-\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}]\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}]\ ,

(S277)

respectively.
Substituting these equations into the interaction Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{U}
, we obtain

{\hat{H}}_{U}\approx{\hat{H}}_{U,MF}=\overline{H}_{U}-E_{U}

(S278)

with

\displaystyle\overline{H}_{U}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\left(U_{1}(\nu_{f}+0.5)+6U_{2}\nu_{f}\right)f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}-U_{1}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s,\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}\beta\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}\ ,

(S279)

and

E_{U}=\frac{NU_{1}}{2}\left(\nu_{f}^{2}+8\nu_{f}-\mathrm{Tr}[O^{f}O^{f}]\right)+3NU_{2}(\nu_{f}^{2}+8\nu_{f}).

(S280)

Following the same logic as decoupling
{\hat{H}}_{U}
, one can decouple
{\hat{H}}_{W}
as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{W}\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{a\eta s}W_{a}\nu_{f}:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:+\sum_{\mathbf{R}a\alpha\eta s}W_{a}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}:\nu_{c,a}-N\sum_{a}W_{a}\nu_{f}\nu_{c,a}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}a}W_{a}\left(O^{cf}_{a\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}+h.c.\right)-N\sum_{a\alpha}\sum_{\eta_{2}\eta_{1}s_{2}s_{1}}|O^{cf}_{a\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}|^{2}W_{a}\ ,

(S281)

where

\nu_{c,a}=\sum_{\eta s}O^{c}_{a\eta s,a\eta s}\ .

(S282)

In the first term we have omitted the Umklapp scatterings,
i.e.
,
W_{a}\nu_{f}:c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:
for
\mathbf{G}\neq 0
, because the state
c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}a\eta s}^{\dagger}
has a huge kinetic energy and the Umklapp scattering will be weak.
We can further organize the terms as

{\hat{H}}_{W}\approx{\hat{H}}_{W,MF}=\overline{H}_{W}-E_{W}\ ,

(S283)

with

\displaystyle\overline{H}_{W}=

\displaystyle\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{a\eta s}\nu_{f}W_{a}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}+\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\nu_{c,a}W_{a}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\eta_{2}s_{2}a}W_{a}\left(O^{cf}_{a\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta_{2}s_{2}}+h.c.\right)

(S284)

E_{W}=N\sum_{a}\left(W_{a}\nu_{f}\nu_{c,a}+4W_{a}\nu_{c,a}\right)+\sum_{a}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}2W_{a}\nu_{f}-N\sum_{a\alpha}\sum_{\eta_{2}\eta_{1}s_{2}s_{1}}|O^{cf}_{a\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha\eta_{1}s_{1}}|^{2}W_{a}\ .

(S285)

We emphasize that the term
\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}2W_{a}\nu_{f}
depends on the cutoff (
\Lambda_{c}
) of the conduction bands and diverges as
\Lambda_{c}\to\infty
.
However, the total energy contributed by
{\hat{H}}_{W}
will converge as
\Lambda_{c}\to\infty
because the divergent part of
\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}2W_{a}\nu_{f}
will be canceled by the divergent part of the first term of
\overline{H}_{W}
.

Then we decouple the exchange interaction
{\hat{H}}_{J}
.
Following the same method as for
{\hat{H}}_{U}
,
{\hat{H}}_{J}
can be approximated as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}\approx

\displaystyle-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{2}}:O^{c}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{2},\alpha+2\eta s_{1}}-J\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\left(O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s_{1},\alpha\eta s_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\right):c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha+2\eta s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha+2\eta s_{1}}:

\displaystyle+NJ\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\left(O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s_{1},\alpha\eta s_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\right)O^{c}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{2},\alpha+2\eta s_{1}}+J\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s_{1},\overline{\alpha}-\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k},\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}

\displaystyle+J\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\overline{\alpha}-\eta s_{2}}O_{\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}\ ;\ \alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}^{c}-NJ\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s_{1},\overline{\alpha}-\eta s_{2}}O_{\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}\ ;\ \alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}^{c}

\displaystyle+\frac{J}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\left(e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}O^{cf}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{2},\alpha\eta s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}+h.c.\right)-NJ\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}O^{cf}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{2},\alpha\eta s_{2}}O^{cf*}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{1},\alpha\eta s_{1}}

\displaystyle-\frac{J}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\left(e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}O^{cf}_{\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}\ ;\ \overline{\alpha},-\eta,s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}+h.c.\right)+NJ\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}O^{cf}_{\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}\ ;\ \overline{\alpha},-\eta,s_{2}}O^{cf*}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{1},\alpha\eta s_{1}}\ .

(S286)

We can organize the terms as

{\hat{H}}_{J}\approx{\hat{H}}_{J,MF}=\overline{H}_{J}-E_{J}\ ,

(S287)

with

\displaystyle\overline{H}_{J}=

\displaystyle-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{2}}O^{c}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{2},\alpha+2\eta s_{1}}-J\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\left(O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s_{1},\alpha\eta s_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\right)c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha+2\eta s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha+2\eta s_{1}}

\displaystyle+J\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s_{1},\overline{\alpha}-\eta s_{2}}c_{\mathbf{k},\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}+J\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\overline{\alpha}-\eta s_{2}}O_{\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}\ ;\ \alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}^{c}

\displaystyle+\frac{J}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\left(e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}O^{cf}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{2},\alpha\eta s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}+h.c.\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{J}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}\left(e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}O^{cf}_{\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}\ ;\ \overline{\alpha},-\eta,s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s_{1}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}+h.c.\right)\ ,

(S288)

and

\displaystyle E_{J}=

\displaystyle-NJ\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s_{1},\alpha\eta s_{2}}O^{c}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{2},\alpha+2\eta s_{1}}-J\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\left(O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha\eta s}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{1}{2}+NJ\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}O^{f}_{\alpha\eta s_{1},\overline{\alpha}-\eta s_{2}}O_{\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}\ ;\ \alpha+2,\eta,s_{1}}^{c}

\displaystyle+NJ\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}O^{cf}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{2},\alpha\eta s_{2}}O^{cf*}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{1},\alpha\eta s_{1}}-NJ\sum_{\alpha\eta s_{1}s_{2}}O^{cf}_{\overline{\alpha}+2,-\eta,s_{2}\ ;\ \overline{\alpha},-\eta,s_{2}}O^{cf*}_{\alpha+2\eta s_{1},\alpha\eta s_{1}}\ .

(S289)

The second term in
E_{J}
diverges as
\Lambda_{c}\to\infty
.
However, the total energy contributed by
{\hat{H}}_{J}
will converge as
\Lambda_{c}\to\infty
because the divergent part of the second term in
E_{J}
will be canceled by the (divergent part of) second term of
\overline{H}_{J}
.
We can equivalently write
{\hat{H}}_{J}
in a more compact form as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{J}=

\displaystyle-\frac{J}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\eta_{1}\eta_{2}s_{1}s_{2}}(\eta_{1}\eta_{2}+(-1)^{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}})f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}O^{c}_{\alpha_{2}+2\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha_{1}+2\eta_{1}s_{1}}

\displaystyle-\frac{J}{2}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\eta_{1}\eta_{2}s_{1}s_{2}}(\eta_{1}\eta_{2}+(-1)^{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}})c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha_{2}+2,\eta_{2}s_{2}}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha_{1}+2,\eta_{1}s_{1}}\left(O^{f}_{\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1},\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{1}\eta_{2}}\delta_{s_{1}s_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{J}{2\sqrt{N}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}\eta_{1}\eta_{2}s_{1}s_{2}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{R}}(\eta_{1}\eta_{2}+(-1)^{\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}})\left(O^{cf}_{\alpha_{2}+2\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}\alpha_{1}\eta_{1}s_{1}}c_{\mathbf{k}\alpha_{1}+2\eta_{1}s_{1}}+h.c.\right)\ .

(S290)

In the end we decouple the Coulomb interaction of the conduction bands.
Usually, it is the Fock term that leads to symmetry breaking in the inner degrees of freedom.
We claim that the symmetry breaking mainly come from the on-site repulsion of the local orbitals and the interaction of conduction bands only renormalize the dispersion of the conduction bands.
Therefore, for simplicity, we will omit the Fock channel of this interaction.
The Hartree-mean field is

{\hat{H}}_{V}\approx{\hat{H}}_{V,MF}=\overline{H}_{V}-E_{V}\ ,

(S291)

with

\overline{H}_{V}=\frac{V(0)}{\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c}\sum_{\eta sa}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}

(S292)

and

E_{V}=\frac{V(0)}{2\Omega_{0}}N\nu_{c}^{2}+\frac{V(0)}{\Omega_{0}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}8\nu_{c}\ .

(S293)

\overline{H}_{V}
only shifts the energy the conduction bands.

In summary, the mean field Hamiltonian of the interaction is given by

{\hat{H}}_{I}\approx{\hat{H}}_{I,MF}=\overline{H}_{U}+\overline{H}_{W}+\overline{H}_{V}+\overline{H}_{J}-E_{U}-E_{W}-E_{V}-E_{J}\ .

(S294)

Since the energy difference between flat-U(4) rotation related states are small (
\lesssim 0.1
meV) there may be strong order-parameter fluctuations at finite temperatures.

.

in the calculation. Other parameters of the single-particle and interaction hamiltonians are given in

S4
and
